The alfa-leetcode-api provides a skill stats endpoint (/:username/skill) which returns topic-level breakdowns. The /progress endpoint isn't separately documented — it's likely the same data as /solved combined with /skill. Let me just implement what we confirmed works and gives us the most value.

Here's the complete update to leetcode_service.py — adding fetch_leetcode_progress using the /solved + /skill combo:

Update services/leetcode_service.py
Add this function:

python
def fetch_leetcode_progress(username):
    """
    Combines solved counts + skill stats into one rich progress object.
    Used for Dashboard stat row and Strength vs Weakness chart.
    """
    solved = _get(f"/{username}/solved")
    skills = _get(f"/{username}/skill")

    if not solved:
        return None

    result = {
        "easy_solved": solved.get("easySolved", 0),
        "medium_solved": solved.get("mediumSolved", 0),
        "hard_solved": solved.get("hardSolved", 0),
        "total_solved": solved.get("solvedProblem", 0),
        "easy_total": solved.get("totalEasy", 0),
        "medium_total": solved.get("totalMedium", 0),
        "hard_total": solved.get("totalHard", 0),
        "topics": []
    }

    if skills:
        # Flatten all skill levels into one list with accuracy score
        for level, weight in [("advanced", 1.0), ("intermediate", 0.75), ("fundamental", 0.5)]:
            for item in skills.get(level, []):
                solved_count = item.get("problemsSolved", 0)
                if solved_count > 0:
                    result["topics"].append({
                        "topic": item.get("tagName"),
                        "solved": solved_count,
                        "level": level,
                        # Estimate accuracy: advanced topics assumed higher accuracy
                        "score": min(round(solved_count * weight * 10), 100)
                    })

        # Sort by score descending
        result["topics"].sort(key=lambda x: x["score"], reverse=True)

    return result

from services.leetcode_service import fetch_leetcode_progress

@platform_bp.route("/leetcode/progress", methods=["GET"])
@jwt_required()
def leetcode_progress():
    user_id = get_jwt_identity()
    user = User.query.get(user_id)
    if not user.leetcode_handle:
        return jsonify({"error": "No LeetCode handle set"}), 400

    progress = fetch_leetcode_progress(user.leetcode_handle)
    if not progress:
        return jsonify({"error": "Could not fetch progress"}), 404
    return jsonify({"progress": progress}), 200
Update dashboard_routes.py to use real LeetCode progress
This is the key change — instead of aggregating stats only from your local problems table, we also pull from LeetCode's API when a handle exists. This gives you accurate counts (28 real problems) even if only 20 are imported in the DB:

python
from services.leetcode_service import fetch_leetcode_progress

@dashboard_bp.route("/stats", methods=["GET"])
@jwt_required()
def get_stats():
    user_id = get_jwt_identity()
    user = User.query.get(user_id)
    problems = Problem.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()
    solved = [p for p in problems if p.status == "Solved"]

    # Try to get real counts from LeetCode API if handle exists
    lc_progress = None
    if user.leetcode_handle:
        try:
            lc_progress = fetch_leetcode_progress(user.leetcode_handle)
        except Exception:
            pass  # fall back to DB counts silently

    # Use LeetCode API counts if available, otherwise use DB counts
    total_solved = lc_progress["total_solved"] if lc_progress else len(solved)
    easy_solved  = lc_progress["easy_solved"]  if lc_progress else len([p for p in solved if p.difficulty == "Easy"])
    medium_solved = lc_progress["medium_solved"] if lc_progress else len([p for p in solved if p.difficulty == "Medium"])
    hard_solved  = lc_progress["hard_solved"]  if lc_progress else len([p for p in solved if p.difficulty == "Hard"])

    accuracy = round((total_solved / (lc_progress["total_solved"] or 1)) * 100, 1) if lc_progress else (
        round((len(solved) / len(problems)) * 100, 1) if problems else 0
    )

    # Topic distribution — prefer LeetCode skill data, fall back to DB
    if lc_progress and lc_progress["topics"]:
        topic_distribution = [
            {
                "topic": t["topic"],
                "count": t["solved"],
                "pct": round((t["solved"] / total_solved) * 100, 1) if total_solved else 0
            }
            for t in lc_progress["topics"][:6]
        ]
        strength_weakness = [
            {"topic": t["topic"], "accuracy_pct": t["score"], "solved": t["solved"]}
            for t in lc_progress["topics"]
        ]
    else:
        # Fall back to DB-derived topic stats
        from collections import Counter
        topic_counter = Counter([p.topic for p in solved if p.topic])
        topic_distribution = [
            {"topic": t, "count": c, "pct": round((c / total_solved) * 100, 1) if total_solved else 0}
            for t, c in topic_counter.most_common(6)
        ]
        topic_stats = {}
        for p in problems:
            if not p.topic:
                continue
            topic_stats.setdefault(p.topic, {"solved": 0, "total": 0})
            topic_stats[p.topic]["total"] += 1
            if p.status == "Solved":
                topic_stats[p.topic]["solved"] += 1
        strength_weakness = [
            {"topic": t, "accuracy_pct": round((s["solved"]/s["total"])*100, 1), "solved": s["solved"]}
            for t, s in topic_stats.items()
        ]
        strength_weakness.sort(key=lambda x: x["accuracy_pct"], reverse=True)

    # Streak from DB daily activities
    activities = DailyActivity.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()
    activity_dates = {a.date for a in activities}
    today = datetime.utcnow().date()
    current_streak = 0
    check_date = today
    while check_date in activity_dates:
        current_streak += 1
        check_date -= timedelta(days=1)

    best_streak = 0
    temp_streak = 0
    prev = None
    for d in sorted(activity_dates):
        if prev and (d - prev).days == 1:
            temp_streak += 1
        else:
            temp_streak = 1
        best_streak = max(best_streak, temp_streak)
        prev = d

    platform_stats = PlatformStat.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()

    return jsonify({
        "total_solved": total_solved,
        "easy_solved": easy_solved,
        "medium_solved": medium_solved,
        "hard_solved": hard_solved,
        "accuracy": accuracy,
        "current_streak": current_streak,
        "best_streak": best_streak,
        "topic_distribution": topic_distribution,
        "strength_weakness": strength_weakness,
        "platform_stats": [ps.to_dict() for ps in platform_stats],
    }), 200
Update ConsistencyHeatmap.jsx to use real calendar data
This is a big upgrade — replace the random data generator with real LeetCode calendar data:

jsx
import { useState, useEffect } from 'react'
import api from '../../services/api'

const WEEKS = 16
const DAYS = 7
const GREENS = ["#1E2235", "#0d4f2f", "#0a7a47", "#10B981", "#34D399"]
const DAY_LABELS = ["", "Mon", "", "Wed", "", "Fri", ""]

function ConsistencyHeatmap() {
  const [calendarData, setCalendarData] = useState({})
  const [tooltip, setTooltip] = useState(null)
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)

  useEffect(() => {
    const fetchCalendar = async () => {
      try {
        const res = await api.get('/platforms/leetcode/calendar')
        setCalendarData(res.data.calendar || {})
      } catch {
        // If no handle or API fails, keep empty (random data fallback below)
      } finally {
        setLoading(false)
      }
    }
    fetchCalendar()
  }, [])

  // Build grid from real calendar data
  // calendarData = { "unix_timestamp": count, ... }
  const buildGrid = () => {
    const today = new Date()
    const grid = []

    for (let w = WEEKS - 1; w >= 0; w--) {
      const week = []
      for (let d = 0; d < DAYS; d++) {
        const date = new Date(today)
        date.setDate(date.getDate() - (w * 7) - (6 - d))
        date.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0)

        // LeetCode timestamps are in seconds, JS Date is in milliseconds
        const timestamp = Math.floor(date.getTime() / 1000).toString()

        // Check nearby timestamps (±1 day) since timezone offsets can shift by a day
        const count = calendarData[timestamp] ||
          calendarData[String(parseInt(timestamp) + 86400)] ||
          calendarData[String(parseInt(timestamp) - 86400)] || 0

        week.push({ count, date })
      }
      grid.push(week)
    }
    return grid
  }

  const grid = buildGrid()

  // Determine color intensity based on count
  const getColor = (count) => {
    if (count === 0) return GREENS[0]
    if (count === 1) return GREENS[1]
    if (count <= 3) return GREENS[2]
    if (count <= 6) return GREENS[3]
    return GREENS[4]
  }

  // Month labels
  const monthLabels = (() => {
    const months = ["Jan","Feb","Mar","Apr","May","Jun","Jul","Aug","Sep","Oct","Nov","Dec"]
    const today = new Date()
    const labels = []
    let lastMonth = -1
    for (let w = 0; w < WEEKS; w++) {
      const d = new Date(today)
      d.setDate(d.getDate() - (WEEKS - w) * 7)
      const m = d.getMonth()
      if (m !== lastMonth) {
        labels.push({ text: months[m], index: w })
        lastMonth = m
      }
    }
    const filtered = []
    for (let i = 0; i < labels.length; i++) {
      if (i < labels.length - 1 && labels[i + 1].index - labels[i].index < 3) continue
      filtered.push(labels[i])
    }
    return filtered
  })()

  return (
    <div className="relative overflow-x-scroll lg:overflow-hidden">
      {tooltip && (
        <div className="absolute z-10 -top-8 px-2 py-1 bg-bg-input border border-border-DEFAULT rounded text-[10px] text-text-primary whitespace-nowrap pointer-events-none"
          style={{ left: tooltip.x, transform: "translateX(-50%)" }}>
          {tooltip.count === 0
            ? "No activity"
            : `${tooltip.count} submission${tooltip.count > 1 ? "s" : ""}`}
          {tooltip.date && ` · ${tooltip.date}`}
        </div>
      )}

      <div className="pl-3.5">
        <div className="relative w-fit">
          {/* Month labels */}
          <div className="relative h-4 mb-1">
            {monthLabels.map((label, i) => (
              <div key={i}
                className="absolute text-text-faint text-[10px] whitespace-nowrap font-medium"
                style={{ left: `${28 + label.index * 18}px` }}>
                {label.text}
              </div>
            ))}
          </div>

          <div className="flex gap-0">
            {/* Day labels */}
            <div className="flex flex-col gap-1 mr-1">
              {DAY_LABELS.map((label, d) => (
                <div key={d} className="h-3.5 text-text-faint text-[10px] leading-3.5 w-6 text-right pr-1">
                  {label}
                </div>
              ))}
            </div>

            {/* Week columns */}
            {grid.map((week, w) => (
              <div key={w} className="flex flex-col gap-1 mr-1">
                {week.map(({ count, date }, d) => (
                  <div
                    key={d}
                    className="w-3.5 h-3.5 rounded-[3px] cursor-pointer transition-opacity hover:opacity-80"
                    style={{ backgroundColor: getColor(count) }}
                    onMouseEnter={(e) => setTooltip({
                      count,
                      date: date.toLocaleDateString("en-US", { month: "short", day: "numeric" }),
                      x: e.currentTarget.offsetParent.offsetLeft + e.currentTarget.offsetLeft + 7
                    })}
                    onMouseLeave={() => setTooltip(null)}
                  />
                ))}
              </div>
            ))}
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>

      {/* Legend */}
      <div className="flex items-center gap-1.5 mt-3 justify-center w-full">
        <span className="text-text-faint text-[10px]">Less</span>
        {GREENS.map((color, i) => (
          <div key={i} className="w-2.75 h-2.75 rounded-xs" style={{ backgroundColor: color }} />
        ))}
        <span className="text-text-faint text-[10px]">More</span>
      </div>
    </div>
  )
}

export default ConsistencyHeatmap

Fix 1: Difficulty — use LeetCode problem details API
The alfa-leetcode-api has a /select?titleSlug= endpoint that returns difficulty for a specific problem. But calling it 20 times sequentially is exactly what's making the dashboard slow. Instead, use their /problems list endpoint which has difficulty for all problems:

Update services/leetcode_service.py:

python
# Cache problem difficulties to avoid repeated API calls
_difficulty_cache = {}

def fetch_problem_difficulty(title_slug):
    """Get difficulty for a single problem by its slug."""
    if title_slug in _difficulty_cache:
        return _difficulty_cache[title_slug]
    
    data = _get(f"/select?titleSlug={title_slug}")
    if data:
        difficulty = data.get("difficulty", "Medium")
        _difficulty_cache[title_slug] = difficulty
        return difficulty
    return "Medium"


def fetch_leetcode_accepted_submissions(username, limit=20):
    """Last N accepted submissions with real difficulty."""
    data = _get(f"/{username}/acSubmission?limit={limit}")
    if not data:
        return []

    submissions = data.get("submission", [])
    seen_slugs = set()
    unique = []

    for s in submissions:
        slug = s.get("titleSlug", "")
        if slug and slug not in seen_slugs:
            seen_slugs.add(slug)
            title = " ".join(word.capitalize() for word in slug.split("-"))
            # Fetch real difficulty
            difficulty = fetch_problem_difficulty(slug)
            unique.append({
                "title": title,
                "title_slug": slug,
                "url": f"https://leetcode.com/problems/{slug}/",
                "difficulty": difficulty,
                "timestamp": s.get("timestamp"),
            })

    return unique

Fix 2: Dashboard slow loading — parallel API calls
The dashboard currently calls /dashboard/stats which internally calls LeetCode API synchronously. Use Python's concurrent.futures to make them parallel:

In dashboard_routes.py:

python
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed

@dashboard_bp.route("/stats", methods=["GET"])
@jwt_required()
def get_stats():
    user_id = get_jwt_identity()
    user = User.query.get(user_id)
    problems = Problem.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()
    solved = [p for p in problems if p.status == "Solved"]

    # Fetch LeetCode data in parallel instead of sequential
    lc_progress = None
    lc_calendar = None

    if user.leetcode_handle:
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as executor:
            future_progress  = executor.submit(fetch_leetcode_progress, user.leetcode_handle)
            future_calendar  = executor.submit(fetch_leetcode_calendar, user.leetcode_handle)
            try:
                lc_progress = future_progress.result(timeout=8)
            except Exception:
                pass
            try:
                lc_calendar = future_calendar.result(timeout=8)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # rest of stats logic unchanged...
This cuts the LeetCode API wait time roughly in half since both calls run simultaneously.

Fix 4: Missing platform cards
Only LeetCode showing means stats.platform_stats only has one entry. That's because Codeforces/CodeChef haven't been synced yet — the platform_stats table only has rows for platforms you've explicitly synced.

In Dashboard.jsx, the fallback Object.entries(PLATFORM_CONFIG) should show all 4 cards when platform_stats.length < 4. Fix the condition:

jsx
// Show all 4 platform slots — synced ones show real data, unsynced show "—"
const platformsToShow = Object.entries(PLATFORM_CONFIG).map(([name, config]) => {
  const synced = stats.platform_stats.find(ps => ps.platform === name)
  return {
    platform: name,
    rating: synced?.rating || "—",
    rank: synced?.rank || null,
    color: config.color,
    ratingChange: 0,
  }
})

// In JSX:
{platformsToShow.map((ps) => (
  <StatCard key={ps.platform} {...ps} />
))}
Fix 5: Streak 0d — DailyActivity not being created on import
When we bulk-import submissions via import-submissions, we create Problem rows but never create DailyActivity rows. So the streak counter sees no activity. Fix in platform_routes.py inside the import loop:

python
from models.daily_activity import DailyActivity

# Inside the import loop, after creating the Problem:
if s.get("timestamp"):
    activity_date = datetime.fromtimestamp(int(s["timestamp"])).date()
    existing_activity = DailyActivity.query.filter_by(
        user_id=user_id, date=activity_date
    ).first()
    if existing_activity:
        existing_activity.problems_solved += 1
    else:
        new_activity = DailyActivity(
            user_id=user_id,
            date=activity_date,
            problems_solved=1
        )
        db.session.add(new_activity)
Then re-run the sync (Settings → Sync LeetCode with clean=true) — this will re-import all problems AND create the correct daily activity entries, fixing both the heatmap and the streak.

Fix 6: DSA Score — make it meaningful
100/100 is meaningless. Replace with a real composite score:

In Dashboard.jsx:

jsx
// Better score calculation
const dsaScore = Math.min(
  Math.round(
    (stats.total_solved / 5) * 0.4 +        // problems solved (max 200 = 40pts)
    (stats.accuracy) * 0.3 +                  // accuracy (30pts)
    (Math.min(stats.current_streak, 30) / 30) * 100 * 0.2 + // streak (20pts)
    (stats.hard_solved / Math.max(stats.total_solved, 1)) * 100 * 0.1 // hard ratio (10pts)
  ),
  100
)
const scoreLabel = dsaScore >= 80 ? "Excellent" : dsaScore >= 60 ? "Good" : dsaScore >= 40 ? "Fair" : "Keep going!"

function ConsistencyHeatmap() {
  
  const [tooltip, setTooltip] = useState(null)
  const [calendarData, setCalendarData] = useState({})
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)

  useEffect(() => {
    const fetchCalendar = async () => {
      try {
        const res = await api.get('/platforms/leetcode/calendar')

        const cal = res.data.calendar || {}

        console.log("First entry:", Object.entries(cal)[0])
        console.log("Total days with activity:", Object.keys(cal).length)

        setCalendarData(cal)
      } catch (e) {
        console.error("Calendar fetch error:", e)
      } finally {
        setLoading(false)
      }
    }

    fetchCalendar()
  }, [])

  const buildGrid = () => {
    const today = new Date()
    const grid = []

    for (let w = WEEKS - 1; w >= 0; w--) {
      const week = []

      for (let d = 0; d < DAYS; d++) {
        const date = new Date(today)
        date.setDate(date.getDate() - (w * 7) - (6 - d))
        date.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0)

        const timestamp = Math.floor(
          Date.UTC(
            date.getFullYear(),
            date.getMonth(),
            date.getDate()
          ) / 1000
        ).toString()

        const count = Number(calendarData[timestamp] || 0)
        week.push({ count, date })
      }
      grid.push(week)
    }
    return grid
  }

  const grid = useMemo(() => buildGrid(), [calendarData])
  
  // Determine color intensity based on count
  const getColor = (count) => {
    if (count === 0) return GREENS[0]
    if (count === 1) return GREENS[1]
    if (count <= 3) return GREENS[2]
    if (count <= 6) return GREENS[3]
    return GREENS[4]
  }

  // Month labels
  const monthLabels = useMemo(() => {
    const months = ["Jan","Feb","Mar","Apr","May","Jun","Jul","Aug","Sep","Oct","Nov","Dec"]
    const today = new Date()
    const labels = []
    let lastMonth = -1
    for (let w = 0; w < WEEKS; w++) {
      const d = new Date(today)
      d.setDate(d.getDate() - (WEEKS - w) * 7)
      const m = d.getMonth()
      if (m !== lastMonth) {
        labels.push({ text: months[m], index: w })
        lastMonth = m
      }
    }
    const filtered = []
    for (let i = 0; i < labels.length; i++) {
      if (i < labels.length - 1 && labels[i + 1].index - labels[i].index < 3) continue
      filtered.push(labels[i])
    }
    return filtered
  }, [])

  return (

Jun 25
Classic 429 — you've hit the rate limit on the free hosted version of alfa-leetcode-api (alfa-leetcode-api.onrender.com). This is a shared public instance and it rate-limits aggressively, especially when you're hitting it multiple times per page load (solved + calendar + skill = 3 calls every dashboard refresh).

The real fix: cache in your database
Instead of calling the external API on every page load, call it once, store the result in your DB, and serve from DB on subsequent requests. Only refresh when the user explicitly clicks "Sync."

This is actually the correct architecture anyway — external API calls should never block a page load.

Step 1: Add cached fields to PlatformStat model
Add to models/platform_stat.py:

python
# Add these columns to PlatformStat
skill_data = db.Column(db.JSON, nullable=True)       # LeetCode skill stats
calendar_data = db.Column(db.JSON, nullable=True)     # submission calendar
last_synced_at = db.Column(db.DateTime, nullable=True) # when was last full sync
Then run:

bash
flask db migrate -m "add cached fields to platform_stat"
flask db upgrade
Step 2: Update to_dict in PlatformStat
python
def to_dict(self):
    return {
        "platform": self.platform,
        "rating": self.rating,
        "max_rating": self.max_rating,
        "problems_solved": self.problems_solved,
        "rank": self.rank,
        "global_rank": self.global_rank,
        "easy_solved": self.easy_solved,
        "medium_solved": self.medium_solved,
        "hard_solved": self.hard_solved,
        "skill_data": self.skill_data,
        "calendar_data": self.calendar_data,
        "fetched_at": self.fetched_at.isoformat() if self.fetched_at else None,
        "last_synced_at": self.last_synced_at.isoformat() if self.last_synced_at else None,
    }
Step 3: Update sync_leetcode to cache everything at once
In routes/platform_routes.py — update the sync endpoint so one "Sync" button press fetches and stores everything:

python
from services.leetcode_service import (
    fetch_leetcode_stats, fetch_leetcode_skill_stats,
    fetch_leetcode_calendar, fetch_leetcode_accepted_submissions
)
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor

@platform_bp.route("/sync/leetcode", methods=["POST"])
@jwt_required()
def sync_leetcode():
    user_id = get_jwt_identity()
    user = User.query.get(user_id)

    if not user.leetcode_handle:
        return jsonify({"error": "No LeetCode handle set"}), 400

    handle = user.leetcode_handle

    # Fetch everything in parallel — one sync, all data cached
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=3) as executor:
        f_stats    = executor.submit(fetch_leetcode_stats, handle)
        f_skills   = executor.submit(fetch_leetcode_skill_stats, handle)
        f_calendar = executor.submit(fetch_leetcode_calendar, handle)

        stats    = f_stats.result(timeout=15)
        skills   = f_skills.result(timeout=15)
        calendar = f_calendar.result(timeout=15)

    if not stats:
        return jsonify({"error": "Could not fetch LeetCode stats. Try again later."}), 429

    stat_row = _upsert_platform_stat(user_id, "LeetCode", stats)

    # Cache skill + calendar data on the stat row
    if skills:
        stat_row.skill_data = skills
    if calendar:
        stat_row.calendar_data = calendar
    stat_row.last_synced_at = datetime.utcnow()

    db.session.commit()
    return jsonify({
        "message": "LeetCode synced",
        "stats": stat_row.to_dict(),
        "skills_cached": bool(skills),
        "calendar_cached": bool(calendar),
    }), 200
Step 4: Update dashboard/stats to use cached data — NO external calls
python
@dashboard_bp.route("/stats", methods=["GET"])
@jwt_required()
def get_stats():
    user_id = get_jwt_identity()
    user = User.query.get(user_id)
    problems = Problem.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()
    solved = [p for p in problems if p.status == "Solved"]

    # Get cached platform stat — NO external API calls here
    lc_stat = PlatformStat.query.filter_by(
        user_id=user_id, platform="LeetCode"
    ).first()

    # Use cached counts if available
    total_solved  = lc_stat.problems_solved if lc_stat and lc_stat.problems_solved else len(solved)
    easy_solved   = lc_stat.easy_solved   if lc_stat else len([p for p in solved if p.difficulty == "Easy"])
    medium_solved = lc_stat.medium_solved if lc_stat else len([p for p in solved if p.difficulty == "Medium"])
    hard_solved   = lc_stat.hard_solved   if lc_stat else len([p for p in solved if p.difficulty == "Hard"])

    accuracy = round((easy_solved + medium_solved + hard_solved) / max(total_solved, 1) * 100, 1)

    # Topic distribution from cached skill data
    topic_distribution = []
    strength_weakness = []

    if lc_stat and lc_stat.skill_data:
        for item in lc_stat.skill_data[:6]:
            topic_distribution.append({
                "topic": item["topic"],
                "count": item["solved"],
                "pct": round((item["solved"] / max(total_solved, 1)) * 100, 1)
            })
            strength_weakness.append({
                "topic": item["topic"],
                "accuracy_pct": item["score"],
                "solved": item["solved"]
            })
    else:
        # Fallback: compute from DB problems
        from collections import Counter
        topic_counter = Counter([p.topic for p in solved if p.topic])
        topic_distribution = [
            {"topic": t, "count": c, "pct": round((c / max(total_solved, 1)) * 100, 1)}
            for t, c in topic_counter.most_common(6)
        ]

    # Streak from DailyActivity
    activities = DailyActivity.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()
    activity_dates = {a.date for a in activities}
    today = datetime.utcnow().date()
    current_streak = 0
    check_date = today
    while check_date in activity_dates:
        current_streak += 1
        check_date -= timedelta(days=1)

    best_streak = 0
    temp_streak = 0
    prev = None
    for d in sorted(activity_dates):
        if prev and (d - prev).days == 1:
            temp_streak += 1
        else:
            temp_streak = 1
        best_streak = max(best_streak, temp_streak)
        prev = d

    platform_stats = PlatformStat.query.filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()

    return jsonify({
        "total_solved": total_solved,
        "easy_solved": easy_solved,
        "medium_solved": medium_solved,
        "hard_solved": hard_solved,
        "accuracy": accuracy,
        "current_streak": current_streak,
        "best_streak": best_streak,
        "topic_distribution": topic_distribution,
        "strength_weakness": strength_weakness,
        "platform_stats": [ps.to_dict() for ps in platform_stats],
    }), 200
Step 5: Update calendar endpoint — serve from cache
python
@platform_bp.route("/leetcode/calendar", methods=["GET"])
@jwt_required()
def leetcode_calendar():
    user_id = get_jwt_identity()
    lc_stat = PlatformStat.query.filter_by(
        user_id=user_id, platform="LeetCode"
    ).first()

    if not lc_stat or not lc_stat.calendar_data:
        return jsonify({"error": "No calendar data. Please sync first."}), 404

    return jsonify({"calendar": lc_stat.calendar_data}), 200
How it works now
User clicks "Sync" in Settings
        ↓
Flask calls LeetCode API (parallel: stats + skills + calendar)
        ↓
Everything stored in platform_stats table
        ↓
Dashboard loads → reads from DB only (instant, no external calls)
Calendar loads → reads from DB only (instant)
Strength chart → reads from DB only (instant)
Dashboard will now load as fast as a normal DB query — no more 429s, no more slow loads. The tradeoff is data is only as fresh as the last sync, which is the correct behavior anyway (LeetCode ratings don't change every minute).

After implementing, go to Settings → Sync LeetCode → then refresh the Dashboard. Everything should load instantly from cache.
